{"type":"assistant","message":{"model":"claude-sonnet-5-5","id":"msg_011CgPNGwagceZNtm4mXtR9D","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"text","text":"=== BEGIN ARTIFACT ARCHITECTURE.md ===\n# ARCHITECTURE: DupFind\n\n| Field       | Value |\n|-------------|-------|\n| Version     | 20261025 V1 |\n| Description | Module layout, package boundaries, and pipeline of the dupfind command-line duplicate file finder. |\n| Depends On  | None |\n| Provides    | go.mod, internal/, cmd/dupfind, bin/ |\n\n## Questions\n\n- None.\n\n## Overview\n\n`dupfind` is a single-binary Go command-line tool. It finds regular files under a directory whose\ncontents are byte-for-byte identical, reports them as a CSV listing or a one-row CSV summary, and\nnever deletes, moves, or modifies any file.\n\nPipeline: parse arguments, validate the directory, walk, group by size, hash same-size groups with\nSHA-256, order and number groups, emit CSV.\n\n## Technology Stack\n\nDerived from `TECHNOLOGY_STACK.md`; that file is the decision of record.\n\n| Technology | Where it applies |\n|---|---|\n| Go 1.22 or newer, standard library only | All application code; `go.mod` declares no third-party requirement |\n| go test and go vet | Test runner behind `bin/test.sh` |\n| POSIX sh | `bin/test.sh` |\n\n## Module Layout\n\n| Path | Package | Responsibility |\n|---|---|---|\n| `go.mod` | module root | Module path and Go toolchain version; no `require` lines |\n| `cmd/dupfind` | `main` | Calls `cli.Run(os.Args[1:], os.Stdout, os.Stderr)` and passes the result to `os.Exit`; the only caller of `os.Exit` |\n| `internal/cli` | `cli` | `Run(args []string, stdout, stderr io.Writer) int`: argument parsing, directory validation, pipeline orchestration, exit codes |\n| `internal/walk` | `walk` | Recursive directory walk yielding eligible regular files |\n| `internal/dupes` | `dupes` | Size grouping, SHA-256 digest grouping, reclaimable bytes, ordering and numbering |\n| `internal/report` | `report` | CSV listing and summary writers using `encoding/csv` |\n| `bin/test.sh` | POSIX sh | Runs `go vet ./...` then `go test ./...` from the application root |\n\n## Boundaries\n\n- Only `main` calls `os.Exit`. `cli.Run` returns the exit code: 0 success, 1 operational failure, 2 usage error.\n- Standard output carries only CSV. A failing command writes nothing to standard output and one diagnostic line to standard error.\n- Warnings (unreadable file or subdirectory) go to standard error only and never change the exit code.\n- File contents are read only by `dupes`, and only for files that share a size with another file.\n- Files under the examined directory are opened read-only. Nothing is written, moved, or deleted there.\n- Application code never reads `sources/run.sh` and never embeds a digest, path, or total.\n\n## Module Ownership\n\n| Boundary | Owning module | Allowed low-level APIs |\n|---|---|---|\n| Directory traversal and file metadata | `internal/walk` | `os.Lstat`, `os.ReadDir`, `io/fs` |\n| File content reading and hashing | `internal/dupes` | `os.Open`, `crypto/sha256`, `io.Copy` |\n| CSV output | `internal/report` | `encoding/csv` |\n| Process arguments and exit codes | `internal/cli` and `cmd/dupfind` | `flag`, `os.Args`, `os.Exit` (main only) |\n| Persistence, configuration files, external services | None; the tool has no persistent state | None |\n\n## Foundation Scope\n\nThe foundation story creates `go.mod`, the package tree above, a `cmd/dupfind/main.go` that\ndelegates to `cli.Run`, and a minimal compiling placeholder in each internal package, so that\n`go build ./cmd/dupfind`, `go vet ./...`, and `go test ./...` succeed before feature stories land.\nThe placeholder `Run` returns 0. `bin/` exists; Exactly one directory argument is required.\n- Usage errors return exit code 2, write nothing to stdout, and write exactly one diagnostic line to stderr. Usage errors are:\n  - no arguments, or an option with no directory;\n  - more than one positional argument, or an option placed after the directory (it counts as an extra argument);\n  - an unknown option, including `-h` and `--help`;\n  - `--min-size` with a missing value, or a value that is not a positive decimal integer.\n- A `--min-size` value is accepted only when it consists solely of ASCII decimal digits, is at least 1, and fits in an `int64`. Signs (`+5`, `-5`), zero, hexadecimal, exponent forms, and overflow are usage errors.\n- Argument errors take precedence over directory validation.\n\n## Reads\n\nNone.\n\n## Writes\n\nStandard error Entries are classified without following links (`os.Lstat` or the `fs.DirEntry` type).\n- Symbolic links are neither followed nor reported, whether they point at files or directories. A directory reachable only through a symbolic link is not traversed.\n- Files whose size is below `minSize` are ignored. Zero-byte files are never returned, even if `minSize` is below 1.\n- Paths use `/` as the separator and no leading `./`.\n- A subdirectory that cannot be read yields one warning line on `warn`, is skipped, and the walk continues. This does not change the exit code.\n- Return order is unspecified; ordering is the responsibility of later stories.\n\n## Reads\n\nDirectory listings and file metadata under the root.\n\n## pipeline of the dupfind command-line duplicate file finder. |\n| Depends On  | None |\n| Provides    | go.mod, internal/, cmd/dupfind, bin/ |\n\n## Questions\n\n- None.\n\n## Overview\n\n`dupfind` is a single-binary Go command-line tool. It finds regular files under a directory whose\ncontents are byte-for-byte identical, reports them as a CSV listing or a one-row CSV summary, and\nnever deletes, moves, or modifies any file.\n\nPipeline: parse arguments, validate the directory, walk, group by size, hash same-size groups with\nSHA-256, order and number groups, emit CSV.\n\n## Technology Stack\n\nDerived from `TECHNOLOGY_STACK.md`; that file is the decision of record.\n\n| Technology | Where it applies |\n|---|---|\n| Go 1.22 or newer, standard library only | All application code; `go.mod` declares no third-party requirement |\n| go test and go vet | Test runner behind `bin/test.sh` |\n| POSIX sh | `bin/test.sh` |\n\n## Module Layout\n\n| Path | Package | Responsibility |\n|---|---|---|\n| `go.mod` | module root | Module path and Go toolchain version; no `require` lines |\n| `cmd/dupfind` | `main` | Calls `cli.Run(os.Args[1:], os.Stdout, os.Stderr)` and passes the result to `os.Exit`; the only caller of `os.Exit` |\n| `internal/cli` | `cli` | `Run(args []string, stdout, stderr io.Writer) int`: argument parsing, directory validation, pipeline orchestration, exit codes |\n| `internal/walk` | `walk` | Recursive directory walk yielding eligible regular files |\n| `internal/dupes` | `dupes` | Size grouping, SHA-256 digest grouping, reclaimable bytes, ordering and numbering |\n| `internal/report` | `report` | CSV listing and summary writers using `encoding/csv` |\n| `bin/test.sh` | POSIX sh | Runs `go vet ./...` then `go test ./...` from the application root |\n\n## Boundaries\n\n- Only `main` calls `os.Exit`. `cli.Run` returns the exit code: 0 success, 1 operational failure, 2 usage error.\n- Standard output carries only CSV. A failing command writes nothing to standard output and one diagnostic line to standard error.\n- Warnings (unreadable file or subdirectory) go to standard error only and never change the exit code.\n- File contents are read only by `dupes`, and only for files that share a size with another file.\n- Files under the examined directory are opened read-only. Nothing is written, moved, or deleted there.\n- Application code never reads `sources/run.sh` and never embeds a digest, path, or total.\n\n## Module Ownership\n\n| Boundary | Owning module | Allowed low-level APIs |\n|---|---|---|\n| Directory traversal and file metadata | `internal/walk` | `os.Lstat`, `os.ReadDir`, `io/fs` |\n| File content reading and hashing | `internal/dupes` | `os.Open`, `crypto/sha256`, `io.Copy` |\n| CSV output | `internal/report` | `encoding/csv` |\n| Process arguments and exit codes | `internal/cli` and `cmd/dupfind` | `flag`, `os.Args`, `os.Exit` (main only) |\n| Persistence, configuration files, external services | None; the tool has no persistent state | None |\n\n## Foundation Scope\n\nThe foundation story creates `go.mod`, the package tree above, a `cmd/dupfind/main.go` that\ndelegates to `cli.Run`, and a minimal compiling placeholder in each internal package, so that\n`go build ./cmd/dupfind`, `go vet ./...`, and `go test ./...` succeed before feature stories land.\nThe placeholder `Run` returns 0. `bin/` exists; `bin/test.sh` itself is delivered by the test-gate story.\n\n## Programmatic Acceptance\n\n=== AC arch-module-no-third-party ===\nIntent: The application root is a Go module that declares no third-party requirement.\n\nimport pathlib\n\ntext = pathlib.Path(\"go.mod\").read_text()\nlines = [ln.strip() for ln in text.splitlines()]\nmodule_lines = [ln for ln in lines if ln.startswith(\"module \")]\ngo_lines = [ln for ln in lines if ln.startswith(\"go \")]\nassert len(module_lines) == 1, text\nassert len(go_lines) == 1, text\nassert \"require\" not in text, text\n=== END AC arch-module-no-third-party ===\n\n=== AC arch-package-tree ===\nIntent: The package directories named by the architecture exist in the build tree.\n\nimport pathlib\n\nfor rel in [\"cmd/dupfind\", \"internal/cli\", \"internal/walk\", \"internal/dupes\", \"internal/report\", \"bin\"]:\n    assert pathlib.Path(rel).is_dir(), rel\nassert pathlib.Path(\"cmd/dupfind/main.go\").is_file()\n=== END AC arch-package-tree ===\n\n=== AC arch-vet-and-build ===\nIntent: The foundation compiles and passes go vet across every package.\n\nimport subprocess\nimport sys\n\nfor cmd in ([\"go\", \"vet\", \"./...\"], [\"go\", \"build\", \"./...\"]):\n    result = subprocess.run(cmd, capture_output=True, text=True)\n    print(result.stdout)\n    print(result.stderr, file=sys.stderr)\n    assert result.returncode == 0, cmd\n=== END AC arch-vet-and-build ===\n\n## User Acceptance\n\n- None.\n\n## Guardrails\n\n- Standard library only; no third-party modules.\n- Never delete, move, or write a file under the directory examined.\n- Do not modify, rewrite, regenerate, or substitute `sources/run.sh`, and never read it from application code.\n- Do not hardcode a digest, a path, or a total; every value is computed from the directory examined.\n- Read a file's contents only when another file has the same size.\n=== END ARTIFACT ===\n=== BEGIN ARTIFACT FEATURE-ArgParsing.md ===\n# FEATURE: Arg Parsing\n\n| Field       | Value |\n|-------------|-------|\n| Version     | 20261025 V1 |\n| Description | Parse --summary, --min-size, and exactly one directory argument, rejecting malformed usage with exit 2. |\n| Depends On  | ARCHITECTURE.md |\n| Provides    | cli.Run, cli.Config |\n\n## Questions\n\n- None.\n\n## Purpose\n\n`cli.Run(args []string, stdout, stderr io.Writer) int` in `internal/cli` interprets the command\nline `dupfind [--summary] [--min-size <bytes>] <directory>` and produces a parsed configuration for\nlater stories.\n\n## Behavior\n\n- Parsing uses a local `flag.FlagSet` with `flag.ContinueOnError` and its own output discarded.\n- `cli.Config` carries `Summary bool`, `MinSize int64`, and `Dir string`. `MinSize` defaults to 1.\n- Options precede the directory. Exactly one directory argument is required.\n- Usage errors return exit code 2, write nothing to stdout, and write exactly one diagnostic line to stderr. Usage errors are:\n  - no arguments, or an option with no directory;\n  - more than one positional argument, or an option placed after the directory (it counts as an extra argument);\n  - an unknown option, including `-h` and `--help`;\n  - `--min-size` with a missing value, or a value that is not a positive decimal integer.\n- A `--min-size` value is accepted only when it consists solely of ASCII decimal digits, is at least 1, and fits in an `int64`. Signs (`+5`, `-5`), zero, hexadecimal, exponent forms, and overflow are usage errors.\n- Argument errors take precedence over directory validation.\n\n## Reads\n\nNone.\n\n## Writes\n\nStandard error only, on usage errors.\n\n## Programmatic Acceptance\n\n=== AC argparse-usage-errors-exit-2 ===\nIntent: Every malformed command line exits 2 with empty standard output.\n\nimport os\nimport tempfile\nfrom drydock_go import cli_runner\n\nwith tempfile.TemporaryDirectory() as d:\n    other = os.path.join(d, \"other\")\n    os.mkdir(other)\n    bad = [\n        [],\n        [\"--summary\"],\n        [\"--min-size\", \"5\"],\n        [d, other],\n        [\"--frobnicate\", d],\n        [\"-h\", d],\n        [\"--min-size\", \"abc\", d],\n        [\"--min-size\", \"0\", d],\n        [\"--min-size\", \"-5\", d],\n        [\"--min-size\", \"+5\", d],\n        [\"--min-size\", \"0x10\", d],\n        [\"--min-size\", \"1e3\", d],\n        [\"--min-size\", \"99999999999999999999999\", d],\n        [\"--min-size\", d],\n        [d, \"--summary\"],\n    ]\n    for args in bad:\n        code, out, err = cli_runner(*args)\n        print(args, code, |\n|-------------|-------|\n| Version     | 20261025 V1 |\n| Description | Exit 1 with empty standard output when the directory argument is missing or is not a directory. |\n| Depends On  | ARCHITECTURE.md, FEATURE-ArgParsing.md |\n| Provides    | cli.validateDirectory |\n| Consumes    | cli.Config |\n\n## Questions\n\n- None.\n\n## Purpose\n\nAfter successful argument parsing, `cli.Run` |\n|-------------|-------|\n| Version     | 20261025 V1 |\n| Description | Walk the directory recursively and yield eligible regular files at or above the minimum size. |\n| Depends On  | ARCHITECTURE.md, FEATURE-DirectoryValidation.md |\n| Provides    | walk.Files |\n| Consumes    | cli.Config |\n\n## Questions\n\n- None.\n\n## Purpose\n\n`internal/walk` enumerates the files that may be considered for duplicate detection. It never opens\nfile contents.\n\n## Interface\n\n```go\npackage walk\n\ntype Entry struct {\n\tPath string // relative to root, \"/\" separated, no leading \"./\"\n\tSize int64\n}\n\nfunc Files(root string, minSize int64, warn io.Writer) ([]Entry, error)\n```\n\n## Behavior\n\n- Traversal is recursive and includes hidden files and directories (names beginning `.`).\n- Only regular files are returned. Entries are classified without following links (`os.Lstat` or the `fs.DirEntry` type).\n- Symbolic links are neither followed nor reported, whether they point at files or directories. A directory reachable only through a symbolic link is not traversed.\n- Files whose size is below `minSize` are ignored. Zero-byte files are never returned, even if `minSize` is below 1.\n- Paths use `/` as the separator and no leading `./`.\n- A subdirectory that cannot be read yields one warning line on `warn`, is skipped, and the walk continues. This does not change the exit code.\n- Return order is unspecified; ordering is the responsibility of later stories.\n\n## Reads\n\nDirectory listings and file metadata under the root.\n\n## Writes\n\nWarning lines on the `warn` writer only.\n\n## Programmatic Acceptance\n\n=== AC walk-rules ===\nIntent: Walk returns hidden and nested regular files, omits symbolic links and zero-byte files, and honors the minimum size.\n\nimport os\nimport pathlib\nimport subprocess\nimport sys\n\nmod = \"\"\nfor line in pathlib.Path(\"go.mod\").read_text().splitlines():\n    if line.startswith(\"module \"):\n        mod = line.split()[1]\nassert mod\n\nGO_TEST = \"\"\"package walk_test\n\nimport (\n\t\"io\"\n\t\"os\"\n\t\"path/filepath\"\n\t\"sort\"\n\t\"testing\"\n\n\t\"MODPATH/internal/walk\"\n)\n\nfunc build(t *testing.T, files map[string]string) string {\n\troot := t.TempDir()\n\tfor name, body := range files {\n\t\tp := filepath.Join(root, filepath.FromSlash(name))\n\t\tif err := os.MkdirAll(filepath.Dir(p), 0o755); err != nil {\n\t\t\tt.Fatal(err)\n\t\t}\n\t\tif err := os.WriteFile(p, []byte(body), 0o644); err != nil {\n\t\t\tt.Fatal(err)\n\t\t}\n\t}\n\treturn root\n}\n\nfunc check(t *testing.T, root string, files map[string]string, min int) {\n\tgot, err := walk.Files(root, int64(min), io.Discard)\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\tt.Fatal(err)\n\t}\n\tvar gotPaths []string\n\tfor _, e := range got {\n\t\tgotPaths = append(gotPaths, e.Path)\n\t\tif int(e.Size) != len(files[e.Path]) {\n\t\t\tt.Errorf(\"size of %s = %d\", e.Path, e.Size)\n\t\t}\n\t}\n\tvar want []string\n\tfor name, body := range files {\n\t\tif len(body) >= min && len(body) > 0 {\n\t\t\twant = append(want, name)\n\t\t}\n\t}\n\tsort.Strings(gotPaths)\n\tsort.Strings(want)\n\tif len(gotPaths) != len(want) {\n\t\tt.Fatalf(\"got %v want %v\", gotPaths, want)\n\t}\n\tfor i := range want \"abc\", \".hid/.x\": \"hidden\", \"sub/deep/f\": \"ff\", \"empty\": \"\", \"b\": \"z\"}\n\troot := build(t, files)\n\tif err := os.Symlink(\"a.txt\", filepath.Join(root, \"link\")); err != nil {\n\t\tt.Fatal(err)\n\t}\n\tif err := os.Symlink(\"sub\", filepath.Join(root, \"dirlink\")); err != nil {\n\t\tt.Fatal(err)\n\t}\n\tcheck(t, root, files, 1)\n}\n\nfunc TestAcceptanceWalkMinSize(t *testing.T) {\n\tfiles := map[string]string{\"one\": \"a\", \"two\": \"ab\", \"three\": \"abc\", \"d/four\": \"abcd\"}\n\troot := build(t, files)\n\tcheck(t, root, files, 3)\n\tcheck(t, root, files, 1)\n}\n\"\"\".replace(\"MODPATH\", mod)\n\ntarget = pathlib.Path(\"internal/walk/zz_acceptance_test.go\")\ntarget.write_text(GO_TEST)\ntry:\n    result = subprocess.run(\n        [\"go\", \"test\", \"./internal/walk\", \"-run\", \"TestAcceptanceWalk\", \"-count=1\", \"-v\"],\n        capture_output=True, text=True,\n    )\n    print(result.stdout)\n    print(result.stderr, file=sys.stderr)\n    assert result.returncode == 0\nfinally:\n    if target.exists():\n        os.remove(target)\n=== END AC walk-rules ===\n\n=== AC walk-vet ===\nIntent: The walk package compiles and passes go
